2026-08-17 · GA-T10 · 7843c3b · [PR #114](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/114) · Admin google-allowlist CRUD + `POST /api/admin/google-login` + the AdminSettings section and AdminLogin button. **MODULE 10 IS COMPLETE** (GA-T1..T10; GA-T11 remains as a deferred follow-up). ⚠ **Sweep membership is the standing trap and it is right:** the **three** allowlist routes joined `ADMIN_ENDPOINTS`; the **two PUBLIC** Google login endpoints (`/friends/auth/google`, `/admin/google-login`) are excluded **with the reason written at the list** — reversing it would either leave an admin route unswept or assert 401 on a route nobody could ever log in through. The GET strips `sub` (`/"sub"/` **zero times**, mutation-verified non-vacuous by replacing the projection). ⚠ **NO auth-mode gate, and that is the row's own constraint** — `auth_mode` governs the **friend** surface only, so GA-T5/T6/T7's gate must NOT be copied here. Verified: the single `auth_mode` line this row adds is the **comment explaining its absence**; pinned positively, with admin Google login returning 200 in legacy mode **in the same test** where the friend endpoint 409s. ⚠ **TWO JUDGEMENT CALLS BEYOND THE SPEC, both upheld with stronger reasoning than proposed.** (1) A verified identity with **no e-mail** is **refused at ADD** (400 `field:'email'`) — not one bad state but **three**: `readAdminGoogleSubs()` already drops such entries, so it would grant nothing at login, be invisible in the GET, **and** be unrevokable; the alternatives require publishing the `sub` (breaking the strip rule this row is pinned on) or a DB edit on an **admin ACL**. (2) Guard order is **`requireAdmin` → `authLimiter`**, the reverse of the public routes — `requireAdmin` returns without calling `next()`, so an anonymous POST **provably never reaches the limiter** and cannot spend the office's shared 20/window, while the api-security sweep reads a deterministic 401 instead of an occasional 429; an authenticated caller is still limited (pinned against a login flood draining the same bucket). ⚠ **Removing the LAST entry is ALLOWED** — password auth is the permanent backup (brief item 3), so Google lockout is recoverable by design and a "cannot remove the last one" guard would protect against a state the design deliberately permits. The password form sits **outside** the `v-if` entirely, so no Google state can hide it. GET/DELETE carry **no** config guard (an operator who unsets `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` must still see and revoke); POST's 503 is honest by contrast — asymmetry pinned in one test. ⚠ **GA-T8's atomicity lesson audited and clean, structurally:** the verify is the **only** `await` and sits **above** a read-modify-write that runs inside one synchronous `db.transaction`; DELETE has no `await`; the login's allowlist read and mint are adjacent, synchronous, and touch **different** settings keys. `mintAdminToken()` is now **shared**, so "byte-identical to password login" is true by construction, and **both** invalidation directions are asserted with a positive probe on the surviving token, not just a 401 on the dead one. ⚠ **A FALSE COVERAGE CLAIM, found in review and fixed properly:** `self-hosted-fonts.spec.js` excluded `/admin` from its external-subresource sweep citing `admin-auth.spec.js` — **which asserts redirect/login/logout only, with no subresource, external-host or CSP assertion**, and classified `/admin/*` as authenticated, untrue of `/admin` itself. That file is **the only thing that catches an un-allowlisted external request on a public route** (the gate sends no security headers — the RD-DS-6 lesson), and `/admin` had become the one public screen outside it. Now swept, visited **anonymously**, with the legacy-gate asymmetry (`/` zero, `/admin` not) **observed on a real target rather than asserted from a stub**. ⚠ `AdminSettings.vue` stays out for the **real** reason — `/admin/settings` genuinely is authenticated, an anonymous visit never mounts the view. ⚠ **THE ACL SCREEN LIED ON A LOAD FAILURE:** a failed allowlist GET left the list empty, rendering **"Zatiaľ žiadny Google účet"** — a positive factual claim that may be false — on the one screen where "nobody has Google access" is the worst thing to show wrongly, with no add control and no explanation. Now an explicit error state; the test seeds a **real** entry first so the empty state would be false *about that instance*. ⚠ **The e-mail is not unique by construction** — `sub` is the key, and two subs can carry the same address (a Workspace address reassigned after deletion, then re-added), giving duplicate list keys and a DELETE that **removes both** with no way to remove one. Now a **409** on an address held by a different sub, decided inside the transaction so nothing is written, with non-vacuity legs (same sub re-adding its own address is still the idempotent 200; revoke-then-add succeeds). ⚠ Honest caveat recorded: the Vue key change is **not independently discriminating** — no reachable state distinguishes it — so the test pins the *invariant*, not the key. ⚠ **A pre-existing coupling fixed in passing:** `bcrypt-nonstring-shape.spec.js` re-adopted its rotated admin token only inside a `DB_PATH`-gated test, so running it **without** `DB_PATH` made four later tests 401 where they expect 400/404 — "reads as someone else's authz regression", the failure this repo keeps paying for. One line; **mutation-verified** both ways. ⚠ **NEW FALSE-GREEN VECTOR, recorded in CLAUDE.md:** `npx playwright test` from the repo **root** resolves a second `@playwright/test` and reports **`Error: No tests found`** — which a `grep ✘` check reads as clean. A GA-T10 mutation probe "passed" that way and proved nothing. **`No tests found` is not a pass**; any wrapper must assert a non-zero test count. Also filed: **FUP-T19** — `POST /api/admin/logout` is **public** and deletes the single `admin_token`, so any anonymous caller can end the admin session (pre-existing; not escalation, but an unauthenticated denial of the admin surface), plus the corrupt-`admin_google_subs` overwrite. Gate: `node --check` clean; **1392 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed on a clean DB** (baseline 1365 + 27).

2026-08-17 · GA-T9 · 9cecdf9 · [PR #113](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/113) · Approval of a Google-attached invitation — the friend INSERT carries the two google columns, the collision 409, the `drop_google_link` re-submit, the dialog Google line and the list badge. ⚠⚠ **THE WHOLE RISK WAS THE TWO IA-T3 INVARIANTS THAT NO TEST CAN CATCH**, and §UC-GA-009 states them precisely because "add Google to approve" reads like licence to add a write or a call. What shipped adds **two COLUMNS to the existing INSERT** — not a statement, not a call. Orchestrator-verified by brace-matching the actual transaction body (lines 642-670): **exactly two write statements** (INSERT friend, UPDATE invitation) and **zero `await`s**; `generateTempPassword`/`hashPassword` at 591-592, outside. bcrypt is ~62 ms against a 0.13-0.21 ms transaction — inside, it would hold the write lock ~400× longer and **nothing in the suite would notice**. ⚠ **The pre-check is stronger than "the transaction rolls back"** (reviewer): it returns **before `getPlaceholderCycleId()`, which can INSERT a placeholder cycle** — so a collision 409 writes **literally nothing**. The constraint layer inside the catch is the second half; the 409 body is one frozen constant `{error, field}` feeding **both** layers, so they cannot drift and no friend can be named. ⚠ **Username-409 precedes Google-409, and that ordering is the RIGHT UX** — a Google-first 409 would invite the admin to discard the link and *then* still hit the username conflict. ⚠ **END-TO-END PROOF, orchestrator-run** (the chain no single spec covers): register with a `TEST:` token → 201 → approve → the friend row carries `google_sub`/`google_email`/`must_change_password: 1` and the 201 body has **no `google_sub`** → on a modern backend that friend's **Google login immediately succeeds**, returning `mustChangePassword: true` (resolved decision #1's forced gate still fires) and `googleLinked: true`. ⚠ **`drop_google_link` is `=== true` ONLY** (GSO-T4 explicit-intent). `'true' / 1 / {} / [] / 'false'` all keep the link and give the retryable 409; non-vacuity pinned with real `true`. The failure direction is the point: **fail-closed costs one retryable 409, fail-open silently destroys an identity the applicant attached.** On a link-less invitation the flag is a deliberate no-op — 400-ing would dead-end an approval in the one state with nothing to dead-end on. ⚠ **THREE COMMENTS RECORDED CORRECT DECISIONS WITH WRONG REASONS** — the recurring defect, and why review keeps paying: the badge comment claimed the Email column is adjacent (the table has **six** columns, Telefón is between, **and that column shows the applicant's CONTACT address, which need not be the Google one**); a test header claimed byte-identity with pre-change behaviour when **both** compared rows come from the new code (it proves `drop_google_link` is a no-op; `invitation-approval.spec.js` passing **unmodified** is the actual baseline); and two controls POSTing the same form had **different disabled gates**, so the secondary action could hand the admin a 400 the primary structurally prevents. ⚠ **Pre-hardened against GA-T10** (reviewer's catch): GA-T10 mints admin tokens via Google login, **rotating the one app-wide admin token**, in this same spec file. `loginAsAdminUI(page)` is now the **first** statement of all four GA-T9 UI tests, so an interleaved GA-T10 test cannot make them 401 and read as a GA-T9 regression. GA-T10's row is armed. **Fallback wording resolved as divergence by AUDIENCE, not by screen**: `Prepojené` on both admin surfaces (verbatim `AdminFriends.vue`), `Google účet je pripojený` kept on `InviteRegister.vue` — a full sentence on a public form for an applicant who has never seen the app. Recorded at both call sites. `google_email` is keyed on the sub, so an address without a sub is never written and never renders as a link — which is exactly what makes **GA-T8's decision to keep the raw sub in `GET /api/invitations` load-bearing**. Non-Google path: `invitation-approval.spec.js` passes **unmodified**, plus a whole-row `SELECT *` comparison excluding only volatile columns **by name**, so a column nobody names cannot drift. The duplicated constraint predicate (rather than importing `friends.js`'s module-private one) is right — **both copies are independently pinned by their own probes**, so a driver message change reddens both. ⚠ **STILL OPEN FOR THE PRODUCT OWNER, untouched:** whether the signed credentials message (07 §UC-IA-006 clipboard + §UC-IA-009 e-mail) should gain "…alebo sa prihlás svojím Google účtom". Ships unchanged. ⚠ **Recorded behaviour worth a copy decision if it ever bites:** the pre-check is deliberately **not** scoped to `active`, matching the partial index — so a **DEACTIVATED** holder still blocks approval and `drop_google_link` is the only way through, with no hint of that in the 409 copy. Gate: `node --check` clean; **1365 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed on a clean DB** (baseline 1352 + 13).

2026-08-17 · GA-T8 · 3da12ef · [PR #112](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/112) · Invite-registration Google attach — optional `google_id_token` on the existing **public** `POST /invitations/register`, courtesy dedupe 409s, the invitation row carrying `google_sub`/`google_email`, and the GIS block on `InviteRegister.vue`. No new endpoint, no migration. ⚠⚠ **THIS ROW BROKE A CODEBASE-WIDE ASSUMPTION AND THE REVIEW CAUGHT IT.** The standing note says non-transactional check-then-write is safe under `instances: 1` **because the handlers are fully synchronous** — and this is the first code to put an `await` (a network call to Google) **between a uniqueness check and its INSERT**. Two registrations racing on the same phone: A yields at the verify, B passes the same dedupe and inserts, A's INSERT hits `idx_invitations_phone_pending` and falls into the generic catch ⇒ **500 on a public endpoint whose contract is a 409**. Fixed at **both** layers (re-check immediately before the INSERT + `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT*`-plus-exact-message → 409, the GSO-T10 pattern that survives a PM2 cluster). ⚠ **The window is unreachable over HTTP here** — `TEST:` tokens resolve in a microtask, which never yields — so a green suite says nothing about it; it is provable only by a direct probe. Recorded in CLAUDE.md as a rule for every future `await`. ⚠ **`node:sqlite` and `better-sqlite3` report DIFFERENT codes for the same violation** (`ERR_SQLITE_ERROR` vs `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE`). The e2e helpers use the former, the routes the latter — **a probe written against the test driver would have "passed" while proving nothing about the route.** The implementer checked against the driver the route actually loads. ⚠ **`ssl.gstatic.com` LOOKED like an RD-DS-6-class CSP gap and is NOT one — `deploy/` correctly untouched.** This row is the **first time the real GIS library has ever run in this suite** (elsewhere stubbed; the gate is legacy so `/` never loaded it), and the button fetches two `ssl.gstatic.com` subresources the production CSP names nowhere. Adjudicated from CSP semantics, not just the (deleted) probe: CSP is enforced **per-document**, a cross-origin `https` iframe gets its own policy from its own headers, and the only mechanisms that push a parent policy into a child (`csp` attribute, local-scheme frames) are ones `renderButton` does not use. ⚠ **The decisive control neither agent had at first: `gsi/style` is in Google's published list PRECISELY BECAUSE it is a parent-context load** — so the list demonstrably distinguishes contexts, and gstatic's absence is informative. `frame-src` is the whole obligation. Also a **latent fix**: `EXTERNAL_ALLOWLIST['/']` gained the host, or a modern+configured staging target would have gone red on a correct page. ⚠ **RESIDUAL — §UC-GA-012's acceptance criterion is still UN-MECHANISED.** "A page rendering the GIS button logs zero `securitypolicyviolation` events" cannot run in CI: the `PROD_CSP` describe aborts every `accounts.google.com` request, and the `BASE_URL` sweep renders against Express, which sends **no security headers**. This row was the first time it was even reachable. Goes to the manual staging walkthrough, **along with real-iframe geometry at 320 px** — the stub renders a `<div>`, not an iframe, so that assertion does not cover real GIS. Both noted in the specs at the points where a reader would assume coverage. ⚠ **NO mode gate, deliberately — breaking the pattern of the previous three rows, and correctly.** GA-T5/T6/T7 all needed one because their endpoint 409s in legacy; **`POST /invitations/register` refuses in no mode**, so a gate would ship the feature **dark on today's legacy prod**. GA-T5's hazard has no analogue: this writes only a new `invitations` row, refuses a sub already on a friend, and needs an admin approval that mints username + temp password regardless of mode — so a legacy-attached identity is a **frozen record that starts working at the flip**, not a dead end. ⚠ Accepted risk recorded in both places: two spec-verbatim strings promise Google login before the flip. **Operator note: set `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` at or after the modern flip** (unset is the feature-off state the block already handles, so the promise is simply never made early). ⚠ **Obligation from GA-T1's review discharged: `GET /api/invitations` keeps the raw `google_sub`, decided and stated at the route** — `requireAdmin`, already returns phone and e-mail (more sensitive than an opaque per-client subject id that authenticates nobody), and **GA-T9 needs it to distinguish linked from unlinked when `google_email` is NULL**. The rejected alternative is written down: strip it and publish the e-mail alone breaks exactly that case, so a future strip must substitute a derived `googleLinked` boolean. No-token path proved three ways (raw `{"success":true}` body, the full stored row field-by-field with both columns NULL, and structurally — the attach sits after every module-07 check); honest caveat that the INSERT **SQL string** changed. Orchestrator-verified independently: no token / `''` → 201 both columns NULL; `123` → **400** `field:'google_id_token'`; garbage → **400** `field:'google'` **not 401** (registration is not a login, the form stays filled); valid → 201 with columns written; duplicate pending sub → 409. Reviewer minors also fixed: `invite-register-shell.spec.js` had been made **network-dependent** (real uncontrolled requests to Google, and its 320 px assertion **vacuous** on a no-egress host) — now stubbed per-file, fulfilling rather than aborting so the view is not measured in its error state; and a comment mis-citing §UC-GA-005 for the 200 px floor, which is GA-T4's measurement, not spec. Two shipped structural counts were **retargeted, not bumped** — bumping would make them flip between two correctly-configured deployments. `invitation-approval.spec.js` passes **unchanged**; `AdminInvitations.vue` untouched (badge is GA-T9's). Gate: `node --check` clean; **1352 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed on a clean DB** (baseline 1333 + 19).

Narrowed: **section visibility on `googleClientId` alone; only the UNLINKED branch gated on modern**, with a counterpart test (a linked friend on transition still sees the section and unlinks with 200). ⚠ **A REAL BUG THE REVIEW FOUND THAT NEITHER AGENT ANTICIPATED:** `onGoogleCredential()` never passed `googleLinked` into `beginSession()`, so on the **one login path where the friend is definitionally linked** the section's state came solely from a **fire-and-forget hydrate documented as allowed to fail silently**. On failure a friend who *just signed in with Google* saw "Prepojte si Google účet…" plus a GIS button, no unlink affordance all session, and — since `hasCredentials` seeds immediately — the **false** banner "Bez hesla sa nebudete môcť prihlásiť…". They just had. Fixed; `true !== false` so §UC-GA-006's prompt is unmoved, stated in the comment. Test reproduces it honestly by **aborting the profile GET**. ⚠ **SCOPE CALL: the set-a-password affordance was DEFERRED, and the reasoning is the row's real output.** GA-T4 predicted GA-T7 would build it. The implementer checked the API instead: `PUT /:id/change-password` **400s** without an existing password (`friends.js:509`) and `POST /:id/setup-credentials` **409s** on `password_hash` **AND** `username` (`friends.js:444`) — **a Google-registered friend fits neither**, so it needs a new credential-minting route with its own security review, not an additive UI section. Retargeted to the real invariant (zero `/heslo/i` buttons in a credential-less friend's dialog) and the GA-T11 row corrected. A pin nobody checks is worse than no pin, because the row claims coverage it does not have. ⚠ Session boundary verified in **both** directions on the six-leak file: one `page.goto`, three in-SPA logins, catching a leaked `true` **and** a leaked `false`, both confirmed against DB rows. Seeds on `typeof === 'boolean'` so an absent field means *undecided* and falls through to the owner-scoped fetch. `google_prompt_dismissed` asserted still `0` after both link and unlink. **Spec amended** (§UC-GA-007, the GA-T2 convention): the gate is derived from **which endpoint each branch calls**, with an instruction to GA-T8/T10 to re-derive rather than copy. 2026-08-16 · GA-T6 · 3501ad7 · [PR #110](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/110) · Post-login link prompt on NeoModal — Áno teraz / Teraz nie / Už sa nepýtať. **Frontend only** (`git diff -- backend/` empty). ⚠⚠ **THE TRIGGER IS STRICT EQUALITY AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ROW.** `googleLinked === false && googlePromptDismissed === false && authMode === 'modern'`. A magic-link login and a legacy shared-password login both **OMIT** those fields, so `!undefined === true` would fire the prompt **for an already-linked friend**, stacked on ML-T6's `showMagicPrompt` — the exact modal-stacking §UC-GA-006's "one modal per login, maximum" forbids. Enforced structurally: `beginSession`'s two params have **no default**, so absent stays `undefined`. The magic-link test is non-vacuous — it asserts `google_sub IS NULL` **and** `google_prompt_dismissed = 0` in the DB *first* (so a truthiness bug **would** have fired), then asserts ML-T6's banner is present and the Google prompt count is 0. ⚠ **`authMode === 'modern'` is NOT in the spec** — added because GA-T5's modern-mode guard means the spec's literal condition would offer a link whose every attempt 409s. Pinned by a **transition-mode** login whose handshake really does carry `false/false` (asserted), then the same backend flipped to modern and the same friend getting the prompt. ⚠ **A REAL BUG THE IMPLEMENTER FOUND UNPROMPTED: `gis.initialize()` registers ONE GLOBAL CALLBACK.** The prompt re-registered it, so after a logout the login card's `googleInitialised` guard skipped `initialize` and its button **rendered perfectly and did nothing** — wired to an unmounted component. No amount of "the button appears" testing catches that; the proof is **firing a credential** at the login card's callback afterwards. Reset lands in `beginSession` synchronously before the child's `setup()`; the reverse direction is safe by construction (`startGoogleLink` calls `initialize` unconditionally and its post-`await` re-read returns early when the modal is gone). **GA-T7's row is armed with the obligation.** ⚠ **Session boundary — all three prohibitions verified** (no module state, no localStorage, no friend-id key), on the file with the **six-leak history**. `:key="sessionSeq"` is bumped *before* `entry.value` is written, so a new handshake genuinely re-creates the instance. After review the A→B **and** B→A legs are both in-instance (exactly one `page.goto` in the whole test) — a reload rebuilds module scope for free and would have laundered the very leak the reverse leg exists to catch. ⚠ **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, deliberately NOT fixed:** an unlinked friend who also has a **pending voucher** gets the prompt painted over the auto-opening voucher overlay (`.modal-layer` z-200 vs a hand-rolled `z-50` teleport), whose buttons are unreachable until the prompt closes. The `!hasPendingVoucher` fix requires the **async** voucher check to resolve, turning the seed into a `watch` — and **seeded-once-at-setup is precisely what makes the boundary safe**. Orchestrator's call: a recoverable z-order overlap is a far smaller cost than re-opening the class behind the six leaks. Recorded where GA-T7 will read it, since that row faces the same temptation. ⚠ **The route sweep restatement found a property NOTHING was asserting** (asked for as the non-trivial half of recording a new GIS importer): an arrival at `/` where the login card **never renders** — a token restore, or `/magic/:token` redirecting into a session. Had the session view loaded GIS on mount, that path would contact Google with **no card and no gesture**, and **`/magic/:token`'s zero was holding only because the sweep uses a failure token**. In `google-auth-library`, `verifyIdTokenAsync` (`oauth2client.js:536-543`) awaits `getFederatedSignonCertsAsync()` at `:540` **before** `verifySignedJwtWithCertsAsync` at `:541` — so on a cold/expired-cache process every junk string at what becomes a **public** endpoint costs an **outbound request to Google**, and the suite would not have been hermetic. Nothing loosened, with **stored-value read-backs** on every case: a full ISO round-trips byte-identically, `YYYY-MM-DD` still normalises to `T00:00:00.000Z`, POST with no `date` still defaults to now, PATCH `null`/`''` still 200-no-ops with `created_at` unchanged, PATCH `{}` still answers `Žiadne údaje na aktualizáciu`, the amount rule still fires first, and `/adjustment` still ignores `date` entirely. Three distinct throws, all measured: `{}` ⇒ `RangeError: Too few parameter values` (better-sqlite3 reads a plain object as a **named**-param map), a multi-element array ⇒ `Too many` (an array argument is **spread**), `true` ⇒ `TypeError: can only bind…`, and `{toString:1}` only through `parseInt`/`parseFloat`. ⚠ **NEW `helpers/bind-value.js` — and `undefined`, not `null`, is the load-bearing return.** better-sqlite3 binds `undefined` as NULL, so on an INSERT an unbindable field stores **byte-identically to an absent one**, while on an UPDATE every gate here is `!== undefined` so it **skips the write rather than wiping**. A `null`-returning guard passes every status assertion and still erases — the FUP-T12 lesson, applied by construction. ⚠ **A shared helper is justified here where FUP-T12 REFUSED one, and the distinction is the point:** there the thrower was a language builtin with no choke point and four divergent falsy semantics; here there is exactly **one** thrower (the binder) and **one** question. The helper carries **no policy** — which branch an "absent" value falls into stays at each call site. ⚠ **THREE SILENT-CORRUPTION FINDINGS, each worse than the 500s this row was opened for:** (1) **a ONE-element array was a silent SUCCESS** — the mirror of FUP-T11's trap: `run(['abc'], a,b,c,d,e)` spreads to exactly the six slots a six-slot statement wants, so `{"name":["abc"]}` returned **201 and stored the bare string `abc`**; (2) `{stock_limit_g:{}}` on PATCH was **silently destructive** — `{}` is truthy, `parseInt({})` is NaN, NaN binds as NULL, so a malformed body answered **200 and removed a product's stock limit** (proved still enforced downstream via `/availability` after the fix); (3) a **half-written product**: `variants:[{price:'abc'}]` inserted the `bakery_products` row and only then 500'd on the variants INSERT, leaving a product with a text price and no variants (reproduced: row id 18, `price='abc'`) — both routes now check every price up front and answer the route's **existing** message. Deliberately NOT changed: `{name: 12345}` still stores `"12345.0"` (a number is bindable, nothing crashes, and tightening it is an unrelated behaviour change); `price_250g:'abc'` still stores `'abc'`; numeric strings still write as numbers. `rewards.js` fixed for **correctness only** — its try/catch logs `e.message`, +72 B, **no stack** — and the comment says so, so nobody later "hardens" it as a log-flood. ⚠ **The implementer caught its own vacuous test and re-verified red:** `/api/bakery-products` is behind `requireAdmin`, so an unauthenticated read-back was comparing `undefined` to `undefined`; all three read-back blocks now assert the seeded values first. Nothing loosened, asserted not assumed: existing status/message/`field` per route, explicit `null` still clears, a real friendId still authenticates and a wrong password still 401s **before** the friend lookup (no new oracle), `_default` still filters, a real bakery snapshot still happens — plus field-by-field **stored-value read-backs** before/after every malformed shape, including proving the friends password **still authenticates** (a blanked credential is invisible to `'' === ''`). Log: **325 probes across 65 fields × 5 shapes append 0 bytes**, with the disallowed-`Origin` counter-assertion still logging. e2e: **+45** in `nonstring-body-shape.spec.js` (129 total), red first at 33 failed / 96 passed. Gate: `node --check` green ×6; orchestrator's independent **1021 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed across 64 files** (baseline 976/4); DB left **legacy**. ⚠ Same-class sites **outside this row's five files** enumerated with routes, auth levels and blockers and recorded as **FUP-T15** — two of them public/token. ⚠ Reconfirmed: on an **accumulated** DB `portal-session-boundary.spec.js` times out (1.2 m) and passes 3/3 in 8.8 s on a fresh one — the documented accumulation effect, not this change.

2026-08-16 · FUP-T11 · 7f6fe65 · [PR #96](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/96) · **The unauthenticated log-flood on `POST /api/admin/login` is CLOSED**, along with the four other live bcrypt non-string 500s. A guard at the bcrypt call would have looked complete while leaving every legacy-hashed instance still 500-ing. Now 401 for both, with byte-identical bodies **and headers** (same `Content-Length`, same `ETag`) across five malformed shapes and a wrong string password. Gate: `node --check` green ×3; orchestrator's independent **892 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed across 63 files** (baseline 835/4); DB left **legacy**. 2026-08-16 · FUP-T9 · 6807d94 · [PR #94](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/94) · `hashPassword()` hoisted out of `db.transaction` in `onboarding.js` + the missing exemption comment — **one file, 21 insertions / 2 deletions, and the suite is an EXACT baseline match (823/4/0)**, which is what a behaviour-neutral change should produce. ⚠ **This CLOSES THE IA-T3 INVARIANT REPO-WIDE:** ML-T7 fixed three `friends.js` sites, this was the fourth, and the reviewer confirmed `admin.js`'s three `bcryptHash` call sites (`:51`, `:77`, `:207`) are all single-statement writes with **no surrounding `db.transaction`** — **there is no fifth site left.** The violation: `hashPassword(password)` was evaluated inline as an argument to the INSERT's `.run()`, putting ~62 ms of blocking bcrypt inside a synchronous better-sqlite3 **write lock** (canon 61.8–64.7 ms against a 0.13–0.21 ms transaction — the lock held ~400× longer than needed). Transaction contents verified byte-identical against `git show HEAD:` apart from the one token; still exactly two writes. Two benign second-order effects the reviewer surfaced: the hoist sits *above* the uid/invite-code collision loops, so the check→INSERT window is now ~62 ms **shorter** than before (an improvement, not a regression), and throw semantics for a non-string password are unchanged (500 either way, no partial write either way — `getPlaceholderCycleId()`'s conditional INSERT was already outside the transaction). ⚠ **THE INTERESTING PART IS THE TEST THAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT WRITTEN, and the reviewer independently agreed.** No HTTP-observable difference exists: `hashPassword` is `bcrypt.hashSync`, pure blocking CPU on the request thread, so request duration and event-loop blocking are identical before and after — **only the SQLite write-lock window moves**, and detecting that needs a second connection racing a ~62 ms gap on an app pinned to `instances: 1` with fully synchronous handlers. Such a test would pass most runs, **which is worse than none**. A "register then log in" test passes before AND after, so it was run as a probe, not shipped. A static-source assertion (`hashPassword(` inside a `db.transaction(` body) **would** be non-vacuous but is a **lint, not a behaviour test**: an honest version needs brace-matching, and shipping it in `e2e/` for this one row would leave four sibling sites fixed under a different precedent. Reviewer's verdict, which I accept: if ever wanted, its home is a repo-wide source check from `package.json` or an ESLint rule covering **every** `db.transaction` body, on **its own backlog row** — a new class of gate, not part of a 21-line hoist. The exemption comment (onboarding is the fourth `password_hash` write site that 09 §UC-ML-009 rule 2 deliberately skips, on 07 approve's grounds) had **both factual claims verified against the schema before being written** — `friends.id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT` (`schema.js:248`) and `login_tokens.friend_id` FK with `foreign_keys = ON` (`:666-674`, `:93`) — so the skipped delete is a no-op **by constraint, not by probability**. That mattered: ML-T7 produced a comment that would have become canon and turned out false, so this one was checked first, and the reviewer confirmed it does not overstate (it claims only that this site is exempt, never that the rule is wrong) and reuses `friends.js:257-258`'s phrasing verbatim rather than asserting something new. Review: **approve, ZERO findings**. ⚠ **Recorded as FUP-T10:** `onboarding.js:218` carries the **third instance** of the bcrypt non-string 500 (`{"password":{"length":12}}` → `Illegal arguments` → 500 + stack), identical in class to the two ML-T6 fixed on `friends.js` — three-for-three now, so it is a repo-wide smell rather than three coincidences, and **this one is on a PUBLIC unauthenticated route**, so the FUP-T3/T7 "no stack on a client-triggerable branch" rule applies. Folded into the same row: `invitations.js:336` says "~100 ms" where the canon and both sibling comments say ~62 ms.

Three changes, **zero backend files**: checkbox `ref(true)` → `ref(false)`; `remember` sent from **both** auth paths (`authenticateFriends` gains it for the legacy shared-password card too — the half most easily forgotten, and explicitly in scope); and **localStorage written on EVERY successful login** — the `rememberMe`-gated `setItem` branches and the in-memory-only fallback retired. The shared-password branch's `if (result.token)` was verified to be the SEC-A1 "we actually have a token" guard, not a disguised remember gate. Pinned five-key stored shape untouched (~25 fixtures write it directly). ⚠ **The security question this row raises, ANSWERED rather than restated:** an unchecked login now persists a session where it previously did not. The bound holds, and **the enforcer is the server, not the client** — `friend-auth.js:82` filters `expires_at > Date.now()` on *every* Bearer request and `createFriendSession` opportunistically deletes lapsed rows, so a stolen payload cannot outlive 24 h; `FriendPortal.vue` is the ONLY real client restore path (`FriendOrder.vue` reads the key but unconditionally delegates via `router.push('/')`); a past `expiresAt` → `removeItem` + login screen (pinned), and a future-dated `expiresAt` over a dead server row → the `beginSession` probe 401s → same fallback. Both replay paths closed. Real delta, and it is the recorded product decision (`09 §Accepted risks`, "Shared/public computers"): on a shared machine an unchecked login now survives tab-close for up to 24 h, and an XSS gets an exfiltratable rather than ephemeral token. ⚠ **Two sanctioned spec edits, both case (a), both named verbatim in §UC-ML-010 item 4, both verified minimal:** `modern-login.spec.js:214` `aria-checked` true→false at rest with the toggle sequence inverted (still exercising pointer→on AND keyboard→off, 3 assertions either side); and the test *"remember-me off keeps the session in memory only"* — which asserted `localStorage` was NULL, a behaviour now **retired** — **retargeted, not deleted**, to *"remember-me off still stores the session — with the 24 h horizon"*, which the reviewer judged **strictly stronger** than the `toBeNull` it replaced. Sweep confirmed complete: `portal-fidelity.spec.js:282` pins only `line-height`; `portal-appbar.spec.js:270`'s `toBeNull` is the **logout** assertion (§UC-ML-007 says logout is unchanged — correctly untouched, and it superficially resembles the retired behaviour); `magic-link.spec.js:1017` is a failed-redemption `toBeNull` (unaffected); every one of the ~25 direct fixtures seeds a **future** `expiresAt`, so the default flip cannot reach them. Two stale comments claiming "remember me defaults on" corrected without touching the assertions beneath them. e2e: **+7** in `magic-link.spec.js` (the file §UC-ML-010 designates), red first at 6 failed / 1 passed — the pre-green being the past-`expiresAt` fallback, correctly, since this row adds no expiry logic. Coverage includes the legacy card driven for real (no auth-mode stub; `bodies[0].friendId` asserted to prove the branch), a real `page.reload()`, and the literal `remember` field on the intercepted POST matched on the **exact** pathname (`/friends/auth-mode` is a prefix collision). Non-vacuity is empirical: the checked cases observed a **>59.99-day** stored horizon, reachable only if `remember:true` actually left the browser. ⚠ A self-inflicted false red is recorded in the spec: an `addInitScript` clear re-fires on `page.reload()` and wiped the payload the reload test exists to check — dropping it is correct (Playwright gives each test a fresh context, so storage already starts empty), not papering over. Review: **approve, 3 minors, all applied in-commit** — all three were **comment-accuracy** defects, which matters here because two of them would have been read as invariants: (1) `api.js`'s `=== true` justification was **circular** (normalising a stray truthy to `false` yields the same 24 h the server would anyway), and unlike ML-T1's server-side pair this third layer is **unpinnable by construction** — both call sites pass a value `NeoCheckbox`/`v-model` already guarantee is boolean, so deleting it leaves the whole suite green; the comment now says so explicitly. (2) The retained-branch annotation claimed "no reachable state" absolutely — but `switchUser()` is client-only, so a **second tab** logging out clears localStorage while this tab still holds the module-scope token, and an in-SPA remount lands exactly there; reworded to "no single-tab flow". (3) `FriendOrder.vue:514`'s *"(when remember me was not checked)"* was the exact stale semantics this row corrected in two spec files — and it sits on the very twin `FriendPortal.vue`'s annotation names, so the two would have contradicted each other. ⚠ **Deliberately NOT done, reviewer-endorsed:** `FriendPortal.vue`'s restore-side `else` reader is dead on the write side (all four writers unconditional) and cannot resurrect a logged-out session (`switchUser` calls `clearFriendsPassword()` in the same breath as `removeItem`), but deleting it would touch the six-leak surface for no behavioural gain — and `FriendOrder.vue` has an identical twin, so **removing one without the other is the wrong shape**; a later row removes both together. Gate: no backend files (so `node --check` had nothing to run); orchestrator's independent **793 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed across 61 files** (baseline 786/4 — delta exactly the 7 new tests) with the frontend rebuilt from source, plus 128 more after the comment fixes; shared DB left in **legacy** mode. ⚠ **ML-T1 + ML-T4 remain a release unit** — the constraint is discharged in the frontend direction, but shipping ML-T1 without this still drops every friend to 24 h. ML-T5 and GA-T4 touch the same login card; no markup or `NeoCheckbox` change here, so both land on top cleanly.

The historical "Full suite after this work: N passed / 3 skipped" lines were deliberately left as records of past runs. (b) *minor:* the banked EM-T2 sparse-`blocks` fix (authorized — EM-T2's own log entry prescribes it for this row) had **no regression net**: `renderViaTemplates` crosses a JSON boundary, so `[undefined]` arrives as `null`, which the pre-existing `!block` check already refused, and all three new cases passed against pre-fix code. The harness now builds a genuine hole **inside the child** (`delete payload.input.blocks[i]`). ⚠ **The implementer's mutation matrix then disproved its own earlier claim** — reverting the **index loop** reddens, but deleting the `undefined` **guard alone stays green**, because with the loop a hole arrives as `undefined` and `!block` catches it. So the loop IS the fix; the guard is redundant and kept only for a better message (`blocks[1] is missing (a hole…)`). Reviewer reproduced the full 3×4 matrix out-of-tree. (c) *minor, and the most interesting:* the e-mail path lowercased **asymmetrically** — JS `toLowerCase()` (Unicode) against SQLite `lower()` (**ASCII-only**) and `trim()` (U+0020 only), so `lower('ŽOFIA@Example.TEST')` = `'Žofia@example.test'` and a friend with an uppercase diacritic in their stored address **could never recover — with nothing able to tell them**, because the enumeration guarantee that makes the endpoint safe is the same thing that hides the dead-end. `findFriend()` now selects active friends with a non-null e-mail and normalises **both sides in JS** (deliberate deviation from §UC-ML-003 step 4's literal SQL, commented at the site, spec amended in this commit, and the stale `friends.js` comment naming the old SQL corrected). The username path deliberately stays on SQL equality — `validateUsername` is `^[a-z0-9._-]+$`, ASCII by construction. ⚠ Mutation-verified, and **how** it fails is the lesson: restoring the old SQL leaves a **byte-identical 200** and only drops the stub call count to zero, so the mail-count assertion is the sole detector. Ambiguous e-mail (≥2 active matches) still ⇒ no match, no mail. e2e: NEW `magic-link.spec.js` (19) + `magic-link-rate-limit.spec.js` (1, self-skipping), red first at 16 failed / 1 passed; +3 sparse cases in `email-templates.spec.js`. Gate: `node --check` green; orchestrator's independent **769 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed across all 59 files**, plus a whole-log audit for 64-hex strings after a full run — **zero**, so the raw token is never logged (it appears only in the stub-captured mail body, which is what ML-T3's e2e will consume). Review: **2 rounds → approve**. ⚠ Pre-existing, out of scope, worth knowing: `GET /api/friends/check-username/:username` is public and unlimited, so the **username** half of the neutrality guarantee is already redundant app-wide — the e-mail half is where the value is. ⚠ **Mail copy is PROPOSED, NOT SIGNED** (UC-ML-006 OPEN): subject, both `text` sentences, all three blocks, plus two new Slovak 400 messages. Also still open: EM-T3's second banked minor (the invalid-`PUBLIC_BASE_URL` boot-line case) is NOT done.

2026-08-15 · ML-T1 · b76eda9 · [PR #87](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/87) · Module 09 opens — `login_tokens` + `friend_sessions.via` + token primitives + the session TTL split (`09 §UC-ML-001,002`). Both have **zero callers in this row** — nothing persists, logs or returns a raw token. ⚠ **The flat 30-day session is RETIRED**: 24 h default / 60 days on `remember === true`. ⚠ **The spec's mint-site inventory was INCOMPLETE — it names four sites, there are five.** `onboarding.js:256` mints an auto-login session on registration and is unlisted; flagged before implementation, and the decision is now recorded **at the site**: it takes the 24 h default because the registration form has no "keep me signed in" control, so there is no opt-in to honour, and assuming 60 days on a form often filled in on a shared office machine is the outcome the split exists to prevent. Seam written down for when the checkbox arrives. Closed in-commit with a subprocess probe that calls the primitive directly with `'false'`/`'true'`/`1` (values no route can produce); **mutation-verified — relaxing only the primitive reddens exactly that test, 1 failed / 2 passed.** Second review minor also applied: `Number.isFinite(opts.expiresAt)` gained `&& opts.expiresAt > Date.now()` — the opportunistic cleanup deletes rows whose expiry has passed, so honouring a past timestamp would delete the row just inserted and still hand back a token authenticating nowhere (unreachable today; the guard now matches what its comment claimed). ⚠ **TTL sweep: nothing broke and ZERO pre-existing spec files were edited** — `session-expiry.spec.js:21` is a lower bound only, and the ~20 fixtures seed a *client-side* `expiresAt` no server value contradicts; independently re-verified by the reviewer (`git log -S` also confirms `friend_sessions` was created once and never altered, so prod genuinely lacks `via`, and the only INSERT is named-column). e2e: NEW `magic-link-schema.spec.js`, **25 tests** (22 + 3 from the review fix), red first at 21 failed / 1 passed — the one pre-green being the response-shape invariant, correctly, since §UC-ML-002 says the shape must not move. The schema/primitive half boots `schema.js` in a **throwaway subprocess** against a temp file, which is also what makes idempotency a real test (same probe twice against one file); 2 tests are `DB_PATH`-gated. ML-T4 supplies the frontend flip; land them in the same release. Existing 30-day rows are correctly left untouched (no bulk update). 2026-08-15 · FUP-T7 · b42cadf · [PR #86](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/86) · Malformed/aborted multipart is 400, not 500-with-a-stack — closes the FUP-T3 → T6 → T7 trilogy. multer raises PLAIN `Error`s (not `MulterError`) for a boundary-less Content-Type, a truncated form, a bad part header, and for `Request aborted`/`Request closed`; carrying neither `status` nor `code`, they were invisible to FUP-T3's status rule AND FUP-T6's code mapping, so they took the 500 branch **with a full stack** — including the ordinary case of an admin's photo upload dropping on a flaky connection. ⚠ **NEW `backend/src/helpers/multipart.js` — a wrapper, deliberately NOT another rule in `index.js`:** there, a bare `Error` with no status is exactly what a genuine fault looks like (the CORS rejection is one; every `throw` in this repo is one), so "bare Error ⇒ 400" would hand a client-error verdict to real bugs app-wide. The missing fact is **which middleware failed**, and only the call site has it. Tagged errors then flow through FUP-T3's existing 4xx branch, so both of its decisions are **inherited, not replicated**. ⚠ **The discriminator is `err.constructor !== Error` → leave (fail closed)** — four structural gates, **zero message matching**, because message matching is the brittle coupling FUP-T3's rule was chosen to avoid (a library copy-edit would silently restore the 500s with the suite green). Reviewer enumerated every throw site in busboy 1.6 / multer 2.2 and confirmed the split: all bad-input and disconnect failures are plain `new Error`, genuine faults are SUBCLASSES (`TypeError`, `RangeError`, `SqliteError`) and keep their 500 + stack. The one plain-`Error` genuine fault in multer (`file-appender.js` "Unknown file strategy") is both unreachable under `.single()` and thrown synchronously outside the try, so it routes to the ROUTE's `next`, not the wrapper's. The helper also **owns the multer instance** (5 MB cap + `memoryStorage` byte-identical to the two deleted copies), so no future upload route can opt out — FUP-T6's argument, applied one level down. ⚠ **`memoryStorage()` is LOAD-BEARING for gate 4** (review minor 1, recorded in-code): `MemoryStorage._handleFile` never errors, but `diskStorage` does, and Node's fs errors (ENOSPC/EACCES/EMFILE) are plain libuv `Error`s arriving through the same callback — a switch to disk storage would report a **full disk as `400 Neplatna poziadavka` with no stack**, the dangerous direction. Gate 4 must grow an `err.syscall == null` exclusion first. Aborts are answered like any other 400: responding to a dead socket is harmless (Node discards the write on a destroyed connection — verified, and the pre-fix 500 body already did exactly that), while swallowing would leave multer's `next(err)` never completing the request and would cost the single log line that is an abort's ENTIRE observable outcome. Log diagnostic is `err.type = req.complete ? 'multipart-malformed' : 'multipart-incomplete'` — a structural fact, nothing client-controlled. e2e: **+14 tests**, red first (4 status + 2 log tests red; the 5 anonymous-401s, the genuine-500-behind-the-parser, the happy path and the "a 500 still logs a stack" counter-assertion green from the start). ⚠ The abort case IS reachable — a raw `node:net` socket, partial body under a large `Content-Length`, then `destroy()` — but is **observable only in the log** (the client is gone; the 400 a half-close returns is Node's own connection response, not ours), so the new `Error-handler logging` describe reads the server's stdout, gated on `SERVER_LOG=<path>` (the `DB_PATH` precedent) and self-skipping without it. **That block is also the first mechanized proof of FUP-T3's no-stack rule, which had never been tested.** Gate: `node --check` green on all 4 backend files; orchestrator's independent **725 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed across all 58 files** with `SERVER_LOG` set (722/6 without it), plus a whole-suite log audit showing exactly **4** stacks — 3 deliberate CORS, 1 deliberate `SqliteError` — and **zero** from multipart. Review: **approve, 3 minors, all applied in-commit** — the `diskStorage` hazard above; the FK counter-assertion's comment corrected (its fault is a SUBCLASS, so it pins "the FK path is still 500" but does NOT constrain the scoping its comment claimed — the scoping is sound *structurally*, the wrapper's callback being multer's `next` rather than the route's, and no e2e can reach that distinction); and `--workers=1`, previously only a comment, now **asserted in `beforeAll`** so it fails loudly instead of flaking (`fullyParallel: false` still runs FILES in parallel at `ceil(cpus/2)`, so on a >2-core box another file's genuine 500 could land inside the log-delta window). ⚠ Baseline correction: this tree had **714** tests before the row, not the 710 recorded on FUP-T6 — the 4 extra are FUP-T6's auth-loop widening, applied after that number was taken. Delta here is exactly +14. Residual, pre-existing, not introduced: the global handler does not check `res.headersSent`, and multer's `Busboy(...)`-throw path calls `next(err)` without setting `isDone`; four abort variants could not produce a second `next(err)` on Node 22, but it is worth remembering if that handler is touched.

2026-08-15 · FUP-T8 · b37be04 · [PR #85](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/85) · ⚠ **CODE SIDE DONE — row deliberately left `[~]`, acceptance needs an operator measurement the sandbox cannot perform.** `client_max_body_size 11m` on `location /api` in both deploy confs. ⚠ **The row's own premise was wrong and the implementer caught it:** the shipped admin UI does NOT upload photos as multipart — `readAsDataURL` in `CycleDetail.vue`/`AdminBakeryProducts.vue` sends a base64 `data:` URI **inside a JSON body**, so the binding cap is `express.json({limit:'10mb'})`, not multer's 5 MB (the only multipart the FE sends is the CSV import). Hence the prod bug was worse than recorded (base64 ≈ 4/3 of the file ⇒ photos failed at ~**750 KB**, not 1 MB) and the "obvious" `5m` fix would have **reintroduced the bug one notch up**, refusing a ~4 MB photo Express accepts. `11m` not `10m` because 10m is byte-identical to `express.json`'s limit (both 10485760, both reject on *exceed*) — nginx would win that race and serve bare HTML where the app has a Slovak JSON 413. Scoped to `/api`: no other location takes a body, so they keep the protective 1 MB default; `client_max_body_size` is single-valued, so the `add_header` inheritance trap documented in the same file does NOT apply. ⚠ **Review found the finding that actually matters (major):** the **NPM edge**'s `10m` is **documented-only and UNVERIFIED**, and §2b of that very doc concludes the Advanced block containing it "was never applied or never saved" — which is exactly why the security headers were moved into the LXC conf. So there are two worlds and only measurement separates them: if the edge is really `10m`, `express.json`'s `entity.too.large` 413 is **publicly unreachable** (byte-identical caps) though multer's 413 stays reachable and nothing legitimate is refused; if the block never applied and the edge is on nginx's **1 MB default**, then **this row fixes nothing** and the photo still dies one hop earlier. Both confs and the NPM doc were corrected in-commit to stop asserting the chain is coherent, and to say plainly that the edge must be measured, not read. Also corrected: the "sitting above every cap *guarantees* the app always answers" claim (false for any public request — the same comment half-retracted itself 11 lines later), the staging conf's uncorrected copy of it, and the multipart-envelope sentence (true, but ~5.25 MB is already ~5 MiB clear of a 10m cap, so it is a note, not a justification for the extra MiB). ⚠ **Structurally untestable:** every e2e spec hits Express on :3997 with no nginx in the path, so **no test was added, deliberately** — a test claiming to cover this would be vacuous (same class as RD-DS-6's "the gate sends no security headers"). `nginx -t` could not be run either (no nginx/docker/podman in the sandbox, and the implementer did not claim otherwise); the real gate is `deploy/deploy.sh:247`, which runs `nginx -t` remotely before `nginx -s reload` under `set -e`. Verified instead: brace-depth 0 and every directive semicolon-terminated in both files, and a sanity batch (`image-upload` + `malformed-body` + `api-security`) **66 passed** with `git diff -- backend/ frontend/` empty. Review: **revise → corrections applied in-commit** (2 major, 4 minor; all six acted on). **Operator procedure to close the row lives in PR #85.**

2026-08-15 · FUP-T6 · 5534e92 · [PR #84](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/84) · Oversized upload returns 413, not 500 — the multer half of FUP-T3. `MulterError` carries `code`/`field` but **no `status`**, so FUP-T3's "preserve any 4xx" rule was blind to it and a >5 MB upload answered a pure client mistake as a server fault (plus a remotely-triggerable full stack in the log, the exact thing FUP-T3's 4xx branch exists to avoid). ⚠ **The design point: the handler GIVES the error a status (`Number(carried != null ? carried : multerStatus(err))`) rather than answering it in a second branch** — so it flows through FUP-T3's existing 4xx branch and **inherits both of that row's decisions for free** (no stack log; `err.message` never echoed — `LIMIT_UNEXPECTED_FILE`'s message quotes the client-supplied field name back at the caller). Copy reuses the existing `CLIENT_ERROR_MESSAGES` map; no parallel mechanism. `LIMIT_FILE_SIZE` → **413**, every other code (`LIMIT_UNEXPECTED_FILE`, `LIMIT_FILE_COUNT`, `LIMIT_PART_COUNT`, `LIMIT_FIELD_*`) → **400**. Placed in the GLOBAL handler, verified rather than assumed: multer is per-route middleware but delivers failures via `next(err)`, and `grep '(err, req, res, next)' backend/src` returns exactly one hit, so all **five** `upload.single(...)` routes (`products.js:43,72,147` + `bakery-products.js:50,191`) already arrive there — enumerated, not sampled, and all five confirmed 413 live. Reviewer walked the status computation exhaustively (carried `0` / `'abc'` / 5xx / `statusCode`, `multerStatus` → `null` ⇒ `Number(null)` = 0, a SqliteError-like `code`-bearing error): nothing 500 becomes 4xx except a `MulterError`, nothing 4xx becomes 500, and FUP-T3's four cases are unmoved. `err.code` joined the warn line only because multer's codes are a fixed 9-value library enum, never client text; `err.field` is client-supplied and deliberately NOT logged, and `req.originalUrl` cannot carry a raw newline (verified), so no log-injection surface was added. ⚠ **Auth boundary pinned across ALL FIVE routes** (review minor 2, fixed in this commit — the shipped test sampled only one): the guard must run before multer or an anonymous caller learns whether a file was too big. `products.js` carries `requireAdmin` inline; **the two bakery routes carry NO inline guard and are protected only by the `requireAdmin` on their MOUNT** (`index.js:92`) — the fragile case, and precisely why one sampled route could not protect the property. e2e: NEW +13 tests in `image-upload.spec.js` (8 shipped + 5 from the widened loop), written RED first — 6 failed / 5 passed before the fix, where the 5 include two counter-assertions (a within-limit upload still 200s; the anonymous 401) that must never be red. Gate: `node --check` green; orchestrator's independent **707 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed across all 58 files** on a freshly seeded DB (baseline 699/3 — delta exactly the 8 shipped tests), plus 66 more after the two review fixes. Review: **approve, 3 minors**, all three acted on rather than waived — the over-strong comment corrected in-commit, the auth loop widened in-commit, and the two residuals recorded as **FUP-T7** (plain multipart `Error`s still 500-with-stack, incl. the ordinary mid-flight abort) and **FUP-T8** (⚠ live prod issue: the container nginx has no `client_max_body_size`, so its 1 MB default silently overrides the app's 5 MB cap and a 1–5 MB photo upload fails in production today — and the e2e gate is structurally blind to it, since every spec hits Express directly). ⚠ Harness: the FUP-T5-era DB-accumulation effect has a practical workaround — run `portal-session-boundary.spec.js` FIRST on a freshly seeded DB (3.3 s); running it last reds its two double-login walks on any full run.

2026-08-15 · FUP-T5 · f51fc85 · [PR #83](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/83) · An unhydrated profile modal wiped a stored Packeta address. `saveProfile()` sent `packeta_address` unconditionally, but the modal seeds it at open from `props.friend`, which arrives via the **fire-and-forget** `hydrateCurrentFriend()` — so opening the modal before that GET landed and saving wrote `null` over a real address. FC-T4 added the open-time-original delta for `phone`/`email` for exactly this reason and left this field out of scope; this row closes it, so `saveProfile` now has ONE rule rather than two. ⚠ **Approach (b) — gating the save on hydration — was rejected as STRUCTURALLY UNABLE to fix it, not merely riskier:** `hydrateCurrentFriend` swallows its error (`FriendPortal.vue:287-289`), so a "hydration **settled**" gate still admits a save whose field is empty and whose write is `null` — the identical wipe; and a "hydration **succeeded**" gate would permanently disable ALL profile saving (including the name) for any friend whose profile GET fails, on a fetch that is non-fatal by design. Record that reasoning before anyone re-proposes the gate. ⚠ **PAYLOAD-SHAPE CHANGE: `name` is now the only key `saveProfile` always sends.** `docs/specification/03-friend-login-portal.md:525` still describes the old `{ name, packeta_address }` body — authorized deviation (this row's own text names the delta as the fix), recorded here in prose rather than by amending 03, following FC-T4's precedent for the same class. `friends-consolidation.spec.js:1036`'s exact-keys pin `['name','packeta_address']` survives **only because that test genuinely fills the address**; a future test that saves without touching it must expect `['name']`. Reviewer walked every trim case and found no combination that silently swallows a real change — the one that could (a stored **whitespace-only** address) is unreachable, because `friends.js:700-702` is the column's ONLY app-wide writer and has normalised `?.trim() || null` since the column's first write (`bd605d2`). Two cases get strictly better: a stale hydrate response landing after a save used to write the stale address back on the next save, and the hydrate-lands-while-open window is now non-destructive in both directions. `profilePacketaOriginal` is seeded per modal OPEN (after the field is assigned) in a component `:key`ed on `sessionSeq`, renders nowhere, and carries nothing across friends — the six-leak rule holds. `FriendOrder.vue:873`'s separate `updateFriendProfile` call is untouched and safe: it is gated on a non-empty address, sends `{ packeta_address }` alone (can only set, never clear), and `order-modals.spec.js:696` still pins that exact single-key payload. e2e: +1 test (`⚠ an unhydrated modal must NOT wipe a stored Packeta address`) that stalls the profile GET, asserts the empty-field precondition, then checks BOTH the wire payload and a server-side re-read — red first, receiving `{name, packeta_address: null}` where `{name}` was expected. **Sanctioned spec edit** (one, minimal): `portal-profile-modal.spec.js:346`'s "blank Packeta address is sent as null, not `''`" keeps its assertion in substance — only its setup changed, because the shared read-only fixture has no stored address, so under the delta a whitespace fill reads as *unchanged*; it now provisions its own friend WITH an address (plus an on-screen non-vacuity check) so the clear is a genuine change. The shared fixture deliberately stays address-less — the prefill tests above assert that field opens empty. Review: **approve, 2 minors** — a stale in-function comment still claiming "the pinned `{ name, packeta_address }` payload is preserved" (fixed in this commit; it was the one that could have led a future reader to reintroduce the wipe) and the 03 staleness (recorded above). No backend change (`git diff -- backend/src` empty).

2026-08-15 · FUP-T3 · 004d035 · [PR #82](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/82) · Global 500 on an unparsable/scalar JSON body → correct 4xx (`07 §Follow-ups item 1`). `express.json` is mounted above every router, so a body it refuses never reaches a route and the global handler in `index.js` reported every such CLIENT mistake as a 500 — app-wide, on every JSON endpoint, reachable unauthenticated. ⚠ **The fix PRESERVES whatever 4xx the error already carries** (`err.status ?? err.statusCode`, 400–499 echoed; everything else keeps 500) rather than flattening to 400 via a `type` allowlist: body-parser has already classified the failure and picked the better status, so `entity.parse.failed` → 400 but **`entity.too.large` → 413** and **`encoding.unsupported`/`charset.unsupported` → 415** (all three were 500 before). The generalisation's load-bearing premise — **no app route or helper throws with a status** (every `throw` is a bare `Error` or a rethrown better-sqlite3 `SqliteError`; no route calls `next(err)` at all) — was verified independently by the orchestrator AND the reviewer, and is stated in the code comment as the invariant it depends on. Reviewer additionally enumerated every third-party source of a status-bearing error in the real chain: `express.static` with default `fallthrough:true` calls `next()` (not `next(err)`) for anything under 500 so it can never deliver a 4xx here at all; `express-rate-limit` answers 429 itself; `cors` rejects with a bare `Error` → still 500 (verified live, which keeps `helpers/credentials-message.js:82`'s documented "a 500 before any route runs" true); `multer`'s `MulterError` carries no status → still 500 (**recorded as FUP-T6**, deliberately not smuggled in here). Free improvement discovered in review: Express's `decode_param` 400 on a malformed `%`-escape (`GET /api/products/%E0%A4%A`) was a 500 too. ⚠ Two deliberate decisions, both to be kept if this handler is ever touched: the 4xx branch **does not log a stack** (any unauthenticated client can trigger it — a stack per hit is a free remote log-flood; it logs one compact `console.warn` with method/URL/`err.type`/status, and for a body-parser rejection the stack pointed into `body-parser/lib/types/json.js` anyway, so nothing diagnostic was lost), and **`err.message` is never echoed** (its text quotes the offending input and names a byte offset). Genuine 500s keep `console.error(err.stack)` byte-identical. ⚠ Consequence, pinned as a decision not an accident: an anonymous malformed body on an **admin** route now returns 400 rather than 401, because the parse failure precedes `requireAdmin`. Verified to be no oracle — the 400 is identical for an existent admin route, a nonexistent API route, a nonexistent nested route and a non-API path, while **well-formed** anonymous requests still get 401; `api-security.spec.js`'s `ADMIN_ENDPOINTS` census sends only well-formed bodies, so that boundary is untouched. Recorded residuals: the SPA fallback's `res.sendFile` can still hand this handler a 412 (bogus `If-Match`, verified live) or a 404 in the `existsSync`→`sendFile` race, answering an HTML route with JSON — but it did that before too as a 500, so only the status changed and both reachable cases are more truthful now; and the 4xx warn line interpolates `req.originalUrl`, which on `/api/guest/:token/…` writes a guest link token into the app log (nginx's `combined` access log already records the full request line on prod and staging, and only the token holder can trigger it — but it is the one new place the app itself writes a URL-borne credential). e2e: NEW `malformed-body.spec.js`, **19 tests**, written RED first — 14 failed / 5 passed before the fix, 19/19 after; the 5 pre-green ones are counter-assertions (valid bodies still reach their routes, CORS still 500) so the fix cannot pass by breaking them. Gate: `node --check` green; orchestrator's independent 106-test batch, then **698 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed across all 57 files** on a freshly seeded DB (baseline 679/3 — delta is exactly the 19 new tests, zero existing specs touched). Review: **approve, ZERO findings**, 3 notes (all recorded above). No UI ⇒ e2e-tester skipped; the 19 API e2e tests are the coverage.

Fixed by hydrating after BOTH fresh-login paths, fire-and-forget, plus a **session-boundary guard** on `hydrateCurrentFriend` itself (pins `sessionSeq` at call time + re-checks `currentFriend.id` before merging) — `switchUser()` does not bump `sessionSeq`, so the id check is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces. Fixed with a **contact-only 401 gate** (`phone !== undefined || email !== undefined` && `owner.friendId == null`), narrower than `requireFriendOwner` and keyed on **presence not truthiness** so a `{email: null}` clear is gated too; costs real users nothing (`/friends/auth` mints a per-friend token in BOTH modes, and `setFriendsPassword()` has no caller anywhere in `frontend/src`). Both fixes mutation-verified. Frontend sends a contact field ONLY when changed, so an admin's concurrent edit is not clobbered and the pinned `{name, packeta_address}` payload shape survives — asserted on the wire. Recorded, non-destructive: a hydrate landing AFTER the modal is open leaves the fields blank for that open (the delta then omits them rather than clearing). e2e: +11 tests (4 API bounds/type-guard/atomicity, 3 UI field-contract/round-trip/error-banner, fresh-login prefill, payload-omission, the legacy-mode 401, and the e2e-tester's hydrate-race leak test that holds A's profile GET open across a logout into B's session). Review: 2 rounds → **approve**, all 4 findings resolved, no new defects. Gate: `node --check` green; orchestrator's own runs 66 + 242 + 393 + 165, then **679 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed across all 57 files** on a fresh DB. ⚠ **Harness finding, NOT a regression:** `portal-session-boundary.spec.js`'s two double-login walks timed out at 30s on the SHARED e2e DB — reproduced identically with FC-T4's source stashed, and they run in **3.2s on a freshly seeded DB** (31.6s → 3.2s). Accumulated friends make the login-list/subscription surface ~10× slower; this is deterministic and data-driven, NOT the "memory pressure, different set each run" hazard CLAUDE.md documents. Re-seed before trusting a slow-walkthrough failure.

2026-08-13 · IA-T4 · a6124c2 · [PR #74](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/74) · The approval dialog — "Vytvoriť" now opens a dialog instead of navigating (the `router.push('/admin/friends?…')` is DELETED; the dead receiver in AdminFriends is IA-T5's). Prefills: username from `inv.username` else a slugify of `inv.name` (NFD → strip combining marks → lowercase → whitespace→`.` → drop outside `[a-z0-9._-]` → clamp 30 → **<3 ⇒ empty**), note from `Pozval/a: {inviter_name}`. Success state swaps the dialog body: mono block with login URL + username + temp password and a copy button whose string is the product-owner-signed ty-form message — verified codepoint-identical to the spec across all three sources (132 chars, single U+002D, no NBSP). ⚠ The dialog is the ONLY holder of the plaintext: no auto-close, no timeout (the one `setTimeout` only flips the copy label), the list refresh runs BEHIND the open dialog, and `confirmApprove` early-returns when `approveResult` is set so a double-click/double-Enter mints exactly ONE friend (proved).
⚠⚠ **E2E FOUND TWO REAL BUGS IN THIS ROW'S OWN FEATURE — both fixed and mutation-verified.** (1) **Keyboard-only "Vytvoriť" silently AUTO-APPROVED.** Enter on the focused trigger fires `keydown` on the BUTTON; Radix synchronously focuses `approve-username` during that same keydown; the **keyup half of the same physical press** then hit the input's `@keyup.enter="confirmApprove"` and approved instantly — minting a real account and a one-time temp password **the admin never saw**, i.e. exactly the failure this module exists to fix. Fixed at the mechanism, not with a timing heuristic: both inputs moved to **`@keydown.enter`**, which is structurally incapable of seeing a phantom keyup whose target was the button, plus an **`event.repeat` guard** for HELD Enter (auto-repeat delivers genuine keydowns to the freshly focused input ~500 ms after open — the keydown switch alone would have accepted them). Deliberate Tab→type→Enter still submits, and that is asserted in the same test as the non-vacuity gate. (2) **Browser Back silently destroyed an uncopied temp password** (SPA navigates, component unmounts, plaintext gone, no warning — §UC-IA-006's "unrecoverable by design" means the explicit close, not an accidental navigation). Fixed with `onBeforeRouteLeave` + native `confirm()` (matching this view's existing `confirm()` conventions); covers the header back chevron too. Reload deliberately unguarded. ⚠ SPEC-HARNESS TRAP recorded by the tester: the Back test CANNOT use `page.goto` — backing out of a document-loaded entry is a real document navigation a vue-router guard never sees, so it would pass for the wrong reason; it navigates in-SPA via the dashboard nav instead.
⚠ SPEC-HARNESS FIX in both e2e files: there is exactly ONE admin token app-wide (`admin.js:85` = `INSERT OR REPLACE … 'admin_token'`), so `loginAsAdminUI(page)` INVALIDATES the token minted in `beforeAll`; both helpers now adopt the browser's token via `localStorage.getItem('adminToken')`. Any future spec mixing UI admin login with API `admin()` calls needs this. Verified pre-existing, masks nothing.
Also closed: the three row actions beside the new button — "Spracované" / "Zamietnuť" / "Vymazať" — had **ZERO e2e coverage anywhere in the suite** before this row (grep-verified); they have it now. Deviations accepted by review: the note is ALWAYS sent (trimmed, possibly `''`) rather than omitted-when-empty, so the endpoint's `note ?? existing` branch is unreachable from the dialog — harmless (nothing in `frontend/src` reads `admin_note`, and §UC-IA-006 prefills from `inviter_name` regardless) and WYSIWYG is right for a field on screen; `api.js`'s `request()` does not surface `err.status`, so 400 and 409 render identically inline, distinguished by the server's Slovak sentence — acceptable, both e2e-covered. Empty-note branch NOT tested: **structurally unreachable** — `invitations.invited_by_friend_id` is NOT NULL with an FK and no `ON DELETE`, and `foreign_keys = ON`, so SQLite RESTRICTs deleting an inviter who still has invitations; recorded in a comment with the condition that would make it real. Gate: 30 tests in invitation-approval (14 API + 16 UI), 83 targeted / 610 full suite, 0 failed. Review: approve, 1 minor (a test title that stated the opposite of its body — fixed). E2E: pass + the two bugs above.2026-08-07 · RD-DS-2 · `task/rd-ds-2` · [PR #42](https://github.com/karolskolar/gorifi/pull/42) · **NeoIcon + BrandChrome, still inert.** Three files under `frontend/src/components/neo/`: `NeoIcon.vue`, `icons.js` and `BrandChrome.vue`. All 14 glyphs were verified **mechanically, not by eye** — the probe re-parses `ui.jsx` lines 5–18 at run time and compares the rendered children attribute-by-attribute — and came back byte-identical ×14, same shape order, no added or dropped attributes, with every default size and `stroke-width` matching the UC-DS-007 table (`check` at 3.6, `gear` at 2). Independently re-verified by the reviewer from a separate harness. Neither `ui.jsx` nor `theme.css` carries `stroke-linecap`/`stroke-linejoin`, so the glyphs render with SVG defaults — confirmed against the prototype. Ticker is **static**: `animationName: none` on `.ticker`, its span and `.hazard`, segment present exactly 3× separated by two U+00A0 verified by codepoint (resolved conflict #1 — the README's "marquee" loses to the canonical stylesheet). Fidelity against the live prototype at 378 px is essentially exact: every measured box identical, and a pixel diff of the chrome band shows 0.25 % of pixels differing by >32/255, all text antialiasing, with the **hazard band diffing zero pixels**. ⚠ `ICONS` lives in its own `icons.js` module, NOT inside `<script setup>` — the reviewer caught that a `<script setup>` literal compiles into `setup()` and is re-allocated **per mounted instance** (confirmed in a minified bundle), and an order screen mounts dozens; after the move the map sits ahead of every `setup()` and no glyph path survives inside one. Unknown-name lookup is `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call`-guarded, so `constructor`/`valueOf`/`toString`/`__proto__` render nothing instead of resolving to a truthy function — the same own-property discipline CLAUDE.md's GSO-T3 scar demands, cosmetic here but a house rule. `BrandChrome` sets `inheritAttrs: false` because the `title` **prop shadows the native `title` attribute** on a multi-root template; verified that `id`/`class`/`title`/listeners land on no rendered element and Vue emits no extraneous-attrs warning. Interpretive call, endorsed by the reviewer as settled by the spec itself: **`#titles` FILLS the `.titles` wrapper rather than replacing it** — the theme styles by descent (`.appbar .titles .t`), so UC-DS-006's own wordmark snippet (a bare `<span class="t">`) would render at body size against a replacing slot. Reviewer verdict **approve**, one round, two minors (one applied, one deferred as a spec decision). Gate re-run by the orchestrator after the fix: `vite build` green, e2e **238 passed / 3 skipped**, zero spec edits — no new e2e spec by design (UC-DS-014 item 6 defers primitive smoke to RD-FL-2). ⚠ **Seam raised into RD-FL-3 as a blocker** (see that row): BrandChrome's fixed 3-slot structure cannot express the portal appbar's clickable `.titles` or its pencil-between-titles-and-`.grow`. Not patched here on purpose — UC-DS-006 calls the structure fixed, so it needs a spec amendment. Also worth knowing before module 03: `title` is `default: ''` rather than `required` (Vue cannot express "required unless `#titles` is used", and `required` would warn on every wordmark screen), and `#titles` overrides both `.t` and `.s`, so a wordmark screen inlines its own `<span class="s">` — which is exactly what `portal.jsx` and `guest.jsx` do. Trap for any future isolated harness: `friends-theme.css` depends on Tailwind preflight's `box-sizing:border-box` (A1 dropped the theme's own reset), so without preflight `.hazard` measures 13 px instead of 10 px.
